In these discussions that have developed in the government, school owners, and the congregation axis, only one dimension of the classroom reality is considered and evaluated accordingly. However, for teachers, students, and other employees, it is easy to say that these discussions have social, cultural, economic, and political dimensions. Several students who have studied in the 11th and 12th grade in public high schools have been subjected to primary high schools, and families have been forced to pay tens of thousands of dollars in financial debt. In the 2015-2016 school year, the MEB announced that each student who attends private upper secondary schools, including primary high schools, will receive a $3,220 education incentive per student. In the 2015-2016 school year, seniors were practically turned into college prep classes, while the cost of admissions to the senior high schools is between 15 and 25,000 euros. Turkey to the chairman of the Grand National Assembly In connection with the Southeastern Anatolia Project (GAP) and the fact that the planned irrigation channels are still unfinished, I move that the Constitution be opened in accordance with Articles 98, 104 and 105 of the Interior Charter. [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] 3) Filiz Kerestecioğlu Demir [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[İzmir]] [[Mardin]] [[Mardin]] [[Mardin]] [[Mwah.]] 11) Burcu Çelik Özkan [[Mwah.]] [[Siirt]] [[Siirt]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Tunceli]] [[Van]] 23) Tuğba Hezer Özturk [[Van]] The Southeast Anatolia Project (GAP) has been set up as a project aimed at improving the income and quality of life for people living in the GAP region, to amend intergalactic differences and contribute to economic development and social stability goals at a national level. The GAP, consisting of two groups, including the Fırat basin and the Dicle basin project, was scheduled to build 22 dams, 19 hydropower plants, and 1 million 822,000 hectares of energy a year as a result of the project's end. The two-stage Southeast Anatolian Project (GAP) has largely ended the construction of hydropower plants, which will meet the country's energy needs, while remains far behind in the process of irrigation canals that will improve its people's economy. As part of the GAP, 9 hydropower plants were completed in 2009 (5,513 MW); 74% of hydroelectric energy projects took place as part of the power plant. Karakaya, Ataturk, Batman, Kinggirl, Dicle, Birekik and Karkamish hydropower plants, which are part of the GAP, have thus far been directly contributing to Turkey's economy. In 2009, the GAP has a 33.7 per cent share of hydroelectric production of 12.1 billion kilowatts of hydroelectric energy produced in Turkey. 1 million 822,000 ha ha ha sites are planned to be watered in the GAP region. As of the end of 2009, a total of 300,397 tranqs were opened to water the area in the Fırat and Tigris basin. At 72,093, the site is still under construction of the irrigation grid and is in the process of planning 1,448,556 areas. Sixteen and a half percent of the irrigation projects are in operation. By the end of 2014, a total of 424,710 tranqs were opened in the Fırat and Dicle basin. In 171,088, the site is still under construction of the irrigation grid and is in the process of planning 1,204,202 areas. 23.6% of the irrigation projects are in operation. As it turns out, in a five-year process, the construction of these channels, which have been made progress in a 7 per cent of the irrigation projects, is moving very slowly. One of the most important things to be concerned about is salting the soil. Due to the lack of education and economic preferences, the use of false irrigation methods that do not conform to the characteristic climate and soil structure of the region and insufficient drainage channels reduce the quality of land and product recolte. According to farmers in the region, the more watering is done, the more fertilizer is given, the more product it's produced. According to the GAP administration, water was planned to be delivered to the region by 2005 at the end of 16 years. However, the irrigation reached only 15 per cent of what was planned, and a significant portion of these areas were salted and then barren because of the use of false water and artificial fertilizer. In the Plains of Harran, salt was first seen in 30,000 hectares, and this area was later barren. After that, salting was seen in 130,000 hectares within 12 years. According to experts, these lands are very difficult to reuse. The irrigation conduits, which will bring the real benefit to the people of the region, must be brought to the energy level of the project, which will not disrupt the ecological balance of the soil as well. For Turkey's contribution to its economic well-being to reflect the well-being of the region's people, the production of irrigation channels must be accelerated. The European Convention on Human Rights defines three articles in a row - 9, 10, 11 - that are indistinguishable freedoms. Freedom of thought and conscience is only meaningful if there is freedom of expression in the next article, or everyone is already free, as a conscience, forever free, if they can express it. In fact, there is freedom of thought and expression. It's called freedom of expression if it can be used. The material that follows is about freedom of assembly. Here, in fact, the institutional structure of freedom of expression is described. The arrangements for demonstration march have been described very clearly in the European Convention on Human Rights, in the courts of the European Court of Human Rights. The peaceful, nonviolent activities of the demonstrators, all kinds of protests, are a clear right, without the need to get permission beforehand. If you give this right and you set up your democracy, your judgment, or you don't tolerate it, then there's no way to talk about freedom of thought in that country, freedom of assembly, freedom of expression. Everyone here, we all know very clearly that Turkey has been sentenced to compensation for Article 11 of the European Convention on Human Rights in hundreds of cases. So Turkey has serious structural problems implementing this contract clause. These problems stem from the enforcement of security guards, but not only that, but I think beyond that, the political will is seriously affected by its approach to this issue. Now, when Esma lost her life in Rabia Square, no matter how unacceptable it was to make a statement, no matter what intent the demonstration was, no matter how it was intended to overthrow, no matter how a child was killed there, no matter how a demonstration was shot by a sniper, no matter how in our human values, no matter how it is in our conscience, no matter whether it was a person who threw an illegal slogan in the demonstrations, no matter whether his political demands were legally justified. With a law proposal for rapid change in the electricity market in Turkey, speeding up investment processes and resolving problems in implementation have been our main goal. We continue our work as the Ministry intensively to reduce external dependence in energy, to meet the energy demand increase that will double in the next ten to fifteen years, and to ensure supply security. The healthy supply issue, a critical factor for energy supply security, is very important. In the past decade, the face of our growing energy sector has been changing dramatically, and the competitive market structure, which has been set up in the effort to liberalise, has been the most important focus of our energy policies. In this context, we have a competitive, transparent energy sector that considers environmental sustainability protected by consumers, and it will continue to be our priority. Our strategies and policies are based on energy supply security, alternative energy sources, diversity of resources, integration of domestic and renewable resources into the economy, sustainability, liberalisation and energy efficiency in energy markets. Ladies and gentlemen of law, when carefully reviewing the proposal, we can clearly see that the main idea is more local and renewable resources and the assessment of this. More domestic and renewable is intended to ensure the supply security of the energy this country needs by reducing global impacts to the minimum. This law proposal will contribute greatly to the reduction of the energy-based foreign trade deficit. As you know, as of 2015, our net energy imports will be at $38 billion, and because of our growing and growing economy, it will be inevitable to increase further over the years. At this stage, the mobilisation of all our domestic resources will allow fewer resources to be transferred abroad in the coming years. In particular, the maximum use of domestic coal resources will make our country's supply security stronger and its foreign political position stronger. Both mining and running a thermal plant will contribute to an employment of about 25,000 people. In the developing world, we now know that renewable energy production portfolios are increasingly important. It is known that a different model of investment is needed to get our resources into the system much faster. So with the YEKA (Renewable Energy Source Area) model, it will be possible to add our renewable energy potential to the system much faster, and a second benefit will be achieved by making the evenings in the home. In fact, this model will also lead to the birth of a new industry that has the ability to finance itself. The ability to produce renewable equipment in our country with this practice will provide regional opportunities for exports to neighbouring countries in later years. Dear lawmakers, our annual gas consumption of 500 million cubic metres of natural gas reached 49 billion cubic metres by the end of 2015 in 1987, when natural gas was used. On the other hand, in 2002, the length of the gas transmission and distribution line, which was 4,510 kilometers [4,510 km], was over 18,000 km [18,000 km] in 2015, when the ongoing lines were completed and taken into operation. More than 13,000 kilometers of this infrastructure alone is caused by the main gas transmission line system. In 2002, natural gas delivered to 5 cities in the industrial and housing area was delivered to our 77 cities and 328 counties as of today. Studies are continuing to ensure that natural gas will be distributed throughout the country, and that other cities and towns will be provided with natural gas. Another important thing to achieve with this law proposal is to increase our storage capacity in order to ensure natural gas supply security. As it is known, natural gas has a significant weight in our energy consumption for both electricity and housing heating. In particular, there is a growing need for warehouses that will contribute to the daily demand for points in winter. We are taking a serious step towards moving supply security to a more advanced point by increasing storage capacity and obligation to 20 per cent, which is already 10 per cent.
